## **SECTION 0 — PREREQUISITES & HOW TO USE**

### **Prerequisites:**

**None.** This is the absolute foundation of the Copywriting Operating System.

### **How to Use This Document:**

**LINEAR READING (First time — 2.5-3 hours):**

Read Parts I-IV sequentially. Internalize the universal laws that never change.

Afterward: Use Part V (checklists) for QC before publishing copy.

**ON-DEMAND CONSULTATION (Experienced copywriters):**

Mini-index → Specific section → Tactic blocks (scan for 30 sec - 5 min).

Summary Boxes (instant recap of each principle).

**PRE-PUBLICATION QC:**

Part V, Section 9.1 (43-item Checklist) — audit the complete copy before launching.

### **About the Hybrid Format:**

This document uses a **maximum-density modular structure**:

✅ Concepts in structured blocks (not continuous narrative prose)  
✅ Strict visual hierarchy (headers, bullets, boxes, tables)  
✅ Density of 1.5-1.6 concepts per conceptual page  
✅ Zero fluff (every word serves a function)  
✅ Narrative is present: Key experiments (Milgram, Asch, Regan — vivid and detailed for memorability)  
✅ Structure is present: Tactics, processes, checklists (organized blocks for execution)

## **PART I — THE 3 FOUNDING AXIOMS**

Before tactics, formulas, templates, swipe files.

There are **3 absolute truths** about human persuasion.

Discovered empirically by masters (1904-1966):

-   John E. Kennedy (1904)
-   Claude C. Hopkins (1923)
-   Eugene M. Schwartz (1966)

Validated scientifically (1974-2011):

-   Robert Cialdini (1984)
-   Daniel Kahneman (2002, Nobel Prize in Economics)
-   Dan Ariely (2008)

**As immutable as the laws of physics.**

They do not change with:

-   Technology (direct mail → email → ads → the unknown future)
-   Culture (Brazil, USA, Japan, Africa — they are universal)
-   Time (1904, 2024, 2100)

**Violate them = Copy fails** (no matter how creative)

**Respect them = Copy works** (even if it's "ugly")

## **SECTION 1.1 — AXIOM 1: ADVERTISING IS SALESMANSHIP IN PRINT**

### **THE DISCOVERY (1904):**

**Historical Context:**

Chicago, 1904. The golden age of American advertising.

Albert Lasker, 24, owner of Lord & Thomas (America's largest agency, with $800k/year in revenue = ~$25M today).

**Problem:** The agency created "beautiful" ads (refined art, elegant prose, impressive layouts).

Clients complained: "The ads don't sell."

Lasker couldn't answer the fundamental question:

**"What IS advertising, anyway?"**

He offered $100 (equivalent to ~$3,000 today) to anyone who could answer it satisfactorily.

Weeks passed. No one could answer.

**The mysterious note:**

May 1904. An envelope arrives at Lasker's office.

A handwritten note:

> _"I am in the saloon downstairs._
> 
> _I can tell you what advertising is._
> 
> _I know you don't know._
> 
> _It will mean much to me to have you know what it is and it will mean much to you._
> 
> _If you wish to know what advertising is, send the word 'yes' down by messenger._
> 
> _Signed: J.E. Kennedy"_

Lasker (intrigued by the audacity) went down immediately.

**The meeting:**

A saloon. John E. Kennedy, 40, a former Canadian mounted policeman, former door-to-door book and Bible salesman.

Ordinary appearance. Extraordinary confidence.

Lasker: "So? What is advertising?"

Kennedy, without hesitation:

> **"Advertising is salesmanship in print. Nothing more. Nothing less."**

Silence.

Lasker processed it (30 seconds that changed the industry).

Then: "Can you prove that?"

Kennedy: "I can. And I will."

Lasker: "You're hired. Start Monday."

**Kennedy became:**

The highest-paid copywriter of the era (1904-1907).

Salary: $28,000/year (when the average American salary = $500/year).

Equivalent today: ~$900,000/year.

**3 words defined modern copywriting:**

**"Salesmanship in print."**

### **THE EXPANSION (Hopkins, 1923):**

Claude C. Hopkins, Kennedy's protégé at Lord & Thomas (worked under Kennedy from 1904-1908), expanded the axiom in _Scientific Advertising_ (1923):

> _"The only purpose of advertising is to sell._
> 
> _Advertising is salesmanship._
> 
> _Its principles are the principles of salesmanship._
> 
> _Successes and failures in both lines are due to like causes._
> 
> _Thus every advertising question should be answered by the salesman's standards."_

And then, even more brutally:

> _"The sole purpose is to make sales. It is profitable or unprofitable according to its actual sales._
> 
> _It is not for general effect. It is not to keep your name before the people. It is not primarily to aid your other salesmen._
> 
> _Treat it as a salesman. Force it to justify itself. Compare it with other salesmen. Figure its cost and results._
> 
> _Accept no excuses which good salesmen do not make. Then you will not go far wrong."_

Hopkins was declaring war on:

❌ **"Brand advertising"** (vague awareness, unmeasurable results)  
❌ **"Creative advertising"** (art for its own sake, not for sales)  
❌ **"Image building"** (general impression without direct conversion)

Hopkins advocated for:

✅ **Advertising = a printed salesman**

✅ **The single metric = sales ($)**

✅ **Absolute traceability** (known cost and results)

And the phrase that terrified the "artists" of advertising:

> _"Fine writing is a distinct disadvantage._
> 
> _So is a unique literary style._
> 
> _They take attention from the subject._
> 
> _Any apparent effort to sell creates corresponding resistance._
> 
> _The best ads ask no one to buy. They are based entirely on service."_

**The brutal translation:**

**"Beautiful" copy that doesn't sell = garbage.**

**"Ugly" copy that sells = gold.**

### **Echoes of the Masters (Temporal Validation — 60+ years):**

**David Ogilvy (1963), 40 years after Hopkins:**

> _"If it doesn't sell, it isn't creative."_

**Gary Halbert (1985), 62 years after Hopkins:**

> _"I don't give a flying fuck if your copy wins the Pulitzer Prize._
> 
> _Did it make money?_
> 
> _No?_
> 
> _Then it's worthless. Period."_

**Consensus of 3 generations** (1923-1985):

**The metric = sales.** Nothing else.

### **THE 5 IMPLICATIONS OF THE AXIOM:**

#### **IMPLICATION #1 — Copy = A Salesman Multiplied Infinitely**

**A personal salesman (physical limitations):**

-   Speaks to 1 person at a time (individual conversation)
-   Daily reach: 10-50 prospects (physical limit — 8h/day, 30 min/prospect = 16 max)
-   Monthly cost: $3,000-10,000 (salary + commission + benefits + training)
-   Scale: Linear (hiring more salesmen = proportional cost)
-   Lifetime: Finite (salesman leaves, knowledge goes with them)

**Copy (salesmanship in print — exponential advantages):**

-   "Speaks" to thousands/millions simultaneously (infinite broadcast)
-   Daily reach: Unlimited (digital = global, 24/7)
-   Cost: One-time creation ($5k-50k one-time, or $0 if you write it)
-   Scale: Marginal (distributing to 1,000 or 1,000,000 = almost identical cost)
-   Lifetime: Infinite (copy keeps selling for decades — a "control" that runs for years)

**Kennedy (1905):**

> _"A good salesman, working hard, can see perhaps 5-10 qualified prospects per day._
> 
> _He might convert 1 in 3._
> 
> _That's 2-3 sales per day._
> 
> _Good copy can achieve the same conversion ratio — but for 100,000 prospects, not 10."_

**The math (a real example):**

**Salesman:**

-   10 prospects/day × 33% conversion × $100 sale = $330/day
-   20 workdays/month = $6,600/month in revenue
-   Salesman cost: $5,000/month
-   **Profit: $1,600/month**

**Copy:**

-   10,000 prospects/day (ad campaign) × 33% conversion × $100 = $330,000/day
-   20 days = $6,600,000/month in revenue
-   Copy cost: $10,000 (one-time) + $50,000 ad spend = $60,000
-   **Profit: $6,540,000/month** (first month)
-   Subsequent months: $6,600,000 - $50,000 ad spend = $6,550,000 (copy already paid for)

**Copy = salesman × 1,000+** (same conversion ratio, infinite reach).

Hopkins:

> _"Multiply the salesman. That is the purpose of advertising."_

#### **IMPLICATION #2 — The Same Laws of Sales Apply to Copy (Identical Structure)**

**A live personal salesman (classic 6-step process):**

**STEP 1 — QUALIFY THE PROSPECT:**

Qualifying questions:

-   "Are you the decision-maker?" (authority to buy)
-   "What is your budget?" (purchasing power)
-   "Do you have a pain/problem my product solves?" (fit)
-   "What's your timeline?" (urgency)

**Purpose:** Don't waste time on the unqualified (no authority, no budget, no pain, no urgency).

**Result:** The salesman focuses only on the qualified (ROI of their time).

**STEP 2 — DIAGNOSE THE NEED:**

Diagnostic questions (SPIN Selling — Rackham):

-   **Situation:** "What's your current situation?"
-   **Problem:** "What's the biggest challenge you're facing?"
-   **Implication:** "If you don't solve this, what happens?" (agitate)
-   **Need-Payoff:** "If you were to solve it, how would things change?" (visualize)

**Purpose:** Understand the SPECIFIC need (don't assume).

**Result:** The salesman knows exactly which pain to solve (personalization).

**STEP 3 — PRESENT THE SOLUTION:**

"Based on what you've told me, I have the perfect solution for you.

Let me explain how it works..."

A customized presentation (based on the diagnosis).

Features → Benefits (translated specifically for their pain).

**STEP 4 — DEMONSTRATE/PROVE:**

"Here's how it works. \[Live or video demo\]

And look at this case study: Client X had the exact same problem you have.

They used our product.

They got Y result in Z time.

Here are the numbers: \[specific data\]."

**Purpose:** Create belief (the product works, it's not just theory).

**STEP 5 — HANDLE OBJECTIONS:**

The salesman listens for signals (body language, hesitation, questions).

Pre-empts the objection before it's verbalized:

"You might be thinking, 'This seems expensive.'

I completely understand.

But consider this: you're currently spending $X/month on a solution that doesn't work (a waste).

Our solution costs $Y/month but solves it for good.

You save $(X-Y)/month = a positive ROI in 60 days."

**Purpose:** Remove friction (objections = barriers to purchase).

**STEP 6 — CLOSE THE SALE:**

"So, does that make sense to you?

Shall we get started?

Sign here. \[Contract\]

Implementation begins Monday. My team will call Tuesday for onboarding."

**Purpose:** Explicitly ask for the action (don't assume the prospect will act on their own).

**Copy (THE SAME structure, translated for print/digital):**

**STEP 1 → HEADLINE:**

Selects a qualified audience.

**Example:**

"For Digital Entrepreneurs Making R$10-50k/Month Who Want to Scale to R$100k+ (Without Working More Hours)"

**Explicit qualification:**

-   Decision-maker: ✅ (entrepreneurs = owners)
-   Budget: ✅ (making R$10-50k = has resources)
-   Pain: ✅ (wants to scale, without more hours)
-   Urgency: ✅ (implicit — "but want to" = now)

**The unqualified scroll past:**

-   Beginners (making <R$10k)
-   The satisfied (happy with R$20k)
-   Those willing to work more (no problem with hours)

**The qualified stop:** "This is for ME."

**STEP 2 → LEAD/PROBLEM SECTION:**

Diagnoses the pain (specific, using the avatar's language — research from Doc 2).

**Example:**

"You've been stuck at R$30k/month for 12+ months.

You work 60+ hours/week. Revenue isn't growing (or it's growing 5-10%, slowly).

You've tried ads (burned R$10k+ with no clear ROI).

You've tried SEO (6 months, position #8 on Google, traffic but no conversion).

You've tried networking (expensive events, exchanged cards, follow-ups that go unanswered).

You feel: Exhausted. Frustrated. Alone.

You question: 'Why can't I scale like so-and-so did?'

Your family asks when things will 'get better.' You don't have an answer.

**It's not your fault.**

The problem isn't effort (you work TOO hard).

The problem is the **system**.

You're using a R$10k system (solo freelancer, ad-hoc tasks) to reach R$100k (structured company, processes, team).

The wrong system for the goal = getting stuck is inevitable."

**Diagnoses:**

-   ✅ Specific pain (stuck at R$30k, 60h/week)
-   ✅ Failed attempts (ads, SEO, networking — future objections identified)
-   ✅ Emotion (exhausted, frustrated, alone — empathy)
-   ✅ Externalizes blame (it's not you, it's the system — Warren's Justify Failures)

**STEP 3 → SOLUTION SECTION:**

Presents the product (connected to the diagnosis).

**Example:**

"Here's what you need: a **Structured Scaling System** (no more isolated tactics).

3 integrated Pillars:

**Pillar 1 — Irresistible Offer:**  
A product/service that sells itself (without you "chasing" clients).  
Correct pricing. Value stack. A guarantee that eliminates risk.

**Pillar 2 — Automated Funnel:**  
Nurtures 24/7 (emails, VSL, webinar). Converts while you sleep.  
Automatic upsells/cross-sells. Maximized LTV.

**Pillar 3 — Predictable Traffic:**  
Consistent leads (optimized ads, organic, partnerships).  
Controlled CAC. 5:1+ LTV:CAC ratio (scalable).

**These 3 together** = how 47 entrepreneurs scaled from R$20-50k → R$100k+ in 6-12 months (without increasing hours — some reduced to 30-40h/week)."

**Presentation:**

-   ✅ Specific name (Structured Scaling System — perceived ownership)
-   ✅ Clear structure (3 pillars — understandable)
-   ✅ Connects to diagnosis ("isolated tactics" diagnosed → "integrated" solution)
-   ✅ Social proof (47 entrepreneurs)
-   ✅ Specific result (R$100k+, 6-12 months, without increasing hours)

**STEP 4 → PROOF SECTION:**

Demonstrates effectiveness (testimonials, data, case studies).

**Example:**

"**Does it work? Here's proof:**

**João Silva, 43, e-commerce, São Paulo:**

Revenue: R$18k/month → R$127k/month (9 months)

Hours: 65h/week → 42h/week

'I applied Pillar 1 (the offer) first. Revenue doubled in 6 weeks.

Then Pillar 2 (the funnel). It doubled again.

Pillar 3 (traffic). It stabilized at R$120-140k/month.

The system works. I was skeptical. The numbers don't lie.'

\[Photo + 90-sec video testimonial + PDF Case Study\]

**Maria Costa, 38, info-product creator, Rio de Janeiro:**

Revenue: R$12k/month → R$98k/month (11 months)

'I was doing everything wrong (isolated tactics without a strategy).

The system gave me clarity. Each pillar was a specific transformation.

Today: a consistent R$100k. I work 35h/week (vs. 70h before).'

\[Photo + Video\]

**Carlos Mendes, 51, B2B consulting, Belo Horizonte:**

Revenue: R$25k/month → R$140k/month (8 months)

'Skeptical at first (I tried 3 "systems" before, none worked).

But I implemented it anyway (no-risk guarantee).

Results in week 3. Today: 5.6x initial revenue.'

\[Photo + Full Case Study\]

**Track record (47 clients, 24 months):**

-   Average increase: 4.7x revenue (6-12 months)
-   Range: 2.1x (minimum) to 12.3x (maximum)
-   Retention: 89% still clients after 12 months (vs. 40-60% industry standard for consulting)

**Guarantee:**

If you implement the complete system (3 pillars, 90 days) and don't reach a minimum of R$50k/month consistently (3 consecutive months):

100% refund + R$5,000 for your time invested."

**Stacked proof** (Doc 8 — 5 Tiers):

-   ✅ Tier 1: Specific testimonials (3, diverse profiles)
-   ✅ Tier 2: Quantified track record (47 clients, 4.7x average)
-   ✅ Tier 3: Case studies (PDFs, detailed)
-   ✅ Tier 4: Implicit credentials (system validated by 47, 89% retention)
-   ✅ Tier 5: Massive guarantee (results-based + penalty)

**STEP 5 → FAQ/OBJECTION HANDLING:**

Pre-empts the main objections (identified in research — Doc 2).

**Example:**

"**You might be thinking:**

**'It's too expensive' ($10,000 investment):**

I understand. It seems like a lot.

But consider:

You've already spent R$10k+ on ads that didn't work, courses that didn't teach, and mentors that didn't deliver.

This system RECOVERS that investment.

You scale from R$30k → R$100k = +R$70k/month.

1 month = a 7x ROI.

After that: Pure profit (an extra R$70k/month = R$840k/year).

Guarantee: If it doesn't work, you get a refund + R$5k (you can't lose, you can only potentially gain).

**'I don't have time' (I already work 60h):**

That's exactly why you need this.

System setup: 40 hours (4 weeks, 10h/week — manageable).

After that: Automation.

The funnel runs on its own. You work LESS (not more).

João (case study above): 65h → 42h (he saved 23h/week by implementing a system that automates).

You don't spend time. You GAIN time.

**'I've tried systems before, they didn't work':**

It wasn't your fault.

Most "systems" are incomplete:

-   Just ads (without an optimized offer = burning money)
-   Just a funnel (without traffic = empty)
-   Just an offer (without a funnel = low conversion)

This one is different: **3 integrated pillars** (it's complete).

Analogy: A 3-legged stool. If one leg is missing, it falls.

You've tried 1- or 2-legged stools. This one has 3 (it holds up).

**'Does it work for my niche?' (specific objection):**

Yes. The 47 clients are in 12 different niches:

E-commerce (8), Info-products (14), B2B Consulting (9), Agencies (7), SaaS (5), Others (4).

The principles (the 3 pillars) are universal.

The application is customized (pillar 1 for e-commerce ≠ pillar 1 for SaaS, but the LOGIC is the same).

\[Show breakdown by niche if space allows\]"

**Pre-empts 4-5 main objections.**

**Handles each one** (ROI, time, belief, niche).

**STEP 6 → CLOSE/CTA:**

Asks for an explicit action (specific, easy, urgent).

**Example:**

"**Ready to scale?**

Here's the next step:

**1\. Click the green button below** ("SCHEDULE STRATEGY CALL")

**2\. Fill out the application** (5 min — questions about your business, so I can prepare a diagnosis)

**3\. Schedule a call with me** (30 min, Zoom, free, zero commitment)

**On the call:**

We'll diagnose your specific business.

I'll identify exactly where you're stuck (which pillar is missing).

**If you're a fit** (ready to implement, committed to results):

You're in. We start Monday.

**If you're not a fit** (wrong timing, misaligned expectations):

I'll tell you honestly. No hard sell.

The call has value EVEN if you don't join (a clear diagnosis = R$2,000 in value, free for you).

**BUT:**

I only accept 12 new clients per quarter (quality > quantity).

**AND:**

This cohort closes **Friday, 11:59 PM Brasília time**.

After Friday: The next cohort is only in March 2025 (a 3-month wait).

**Spots:** 12 total. Filled: 7. **Remaining: 5.**

**Don't miss your window.**

\[GREEN BUTTON: SCHEDULE STRATEGY CALL NOW\]

**P.S.** The call is free, with no obligation. Even if you decide not to join, you'll leave with a clear diagnosis of what's holding your business back (real value).

Worst case: 30 minutes for a free diagnosis.

Best case: You join and scale to R$100k+ in the next 6-12 months.

Zero downside. Click now."

**CTA breakdown:**

-   ✅ Specific command (click button, fill out, schedule)
-   ✅ Clear process (3 numbered steps)
-   ✅ Ease factor ("5 min application," "30 min call," "free")
-   ✅ Benefit reminder (R$2k value diagnosis, potential for R$100k+ scaling)
-   ✅ Risk reversal (zero commitment, value even if you don't join)
-   ✅ Real urgency (cohort closes Friday, scarcity of 5 spots)
-   ✅ P.S. (reinforces zero-risk + upside)

### **COMPARATIVE ANALYSIS (Salesman vs. Copy):**

| Element | Salesman (Live) | Copy (Print/Digital) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Qualify** | Verbal questions | Headline (self-selection) |
| **Diagnose** | SPIN questions | Lead/Problem section |
| **Present** | Customized pitch | Solution section |
| **Prove** | Demo + cases | Proof section (testimonials, data) |
| **Objections** | Responds in real-time | FAQ (pre-empts top 5) |
| **Close** | Asks for signature | CTA (specific command) |

**IDENTICAL STRUCTURE.**

**6 salesman steps = 6 copy sections.**

**Copy that doesn't follow this structure = is not salesmanship = violates Axiom 1 = fails.**

#### **IMPLICATION #3 — The Only Metric is Sales (Not Creativity, Awards, or Opinions)**

Hopkins (1923), crystal clear:

> _"Advertising, like salesmanship, must be judged by ONE standard:_
> 
> _Does it sell?_
> 
> _Not by opinions. Not by arguments. Not by applause._
> 
> _Sales are the only test."_

**It doesn't matter:**

❌ Aesthetic beauty (pretty design)  
❌ Creative awards (Cannes Lions, One Show)  
❌ Literary originality (unique prose)  
❌ "Buzz" or virality (views without sales)  
❌ Praise from peers ("Amazing copy!")  
❌ Client approval ("I love it!" without buying)

**It matters:**

✅ **$ (or R$, €, £) in sales generated**  
✅ **Conversion rate %** (how many bought ÷ how many saw)  
✅ **CAC** (Customer Acquisition Cost — how much each customer cost)  
✅ **LTV** (Lifetime Value — how much a customer is worth in total)  
✅ **LTV:CAC ratio** (business health — aim for 5:1+)  
✅ **ROI** (return on investment in copy/ads)

Hopkins (even more brutally):

> _"We are not interested in opinions — either ours or others._
> 
> _We are interested in FACTS._
> 
> _Sales produced by each advertisement are the facts we want."_

**Ogilvy (1963), 40 years later, echoing:**

> _"When I write an advertisement, I don't want you to tell me that you find it 'creative.'_
> 
> _I want you to find it so interesting that you buy the product._
> 
> _When Aeschines spoke, they said, 'How well he speaks.'_
> 
> _But when Demosthenes spoke, they said, 'Let us march against Philip.'_
> 
> _I'm for Demosthenes."_

And the definitive phrase:

> _"If it doesn't sell, it isn't creative."_

**Halbert (1985), 62 years after Hopkins, unfiltered:**

> _"I don't give a rat's ass if your copy wins the fucking Pulitzer Prize._
> 
> _Did it make money?_
> 
> _No?_
> 
> _Then it's worthless. It's shit. Throw it away and start over."_

**Absolute consensus** (3 generations, 1923-1985):

**Copy exists to SELL.**

**The metric = $$$**

**Everything else = vanity.**

**Practical implication:**

**Common scenario:**

You: "Which copy should we use, A or B?"

Client: "I like A more. It's more beautiful. Superior design. Elegant prose."

You: "Let's test it."

**The test (split-run, 200 people each):**

Copy A ("beautiful"): 2.1% conversion (4 sales, $400 revenue)

Copy B ("simple"): 5.3% conversion (11 sales, $1,100 revenue)

**The decision:**

B wins (it doesn't matter that A is "more beautiful").

Client: "But A has a better design... I prefer A..."

You (channeling Hopkins/Ogilvy/Halbert):

**"The MARKET preferred B.**

**B sold 2.75x more.**

**B generated $700 more in revenue.**

**Your opinion ≠ reality.**

**My opinion ≠ reality.**

**SALES = reality.**

**We're using B."**

Hopkins:

> _"The court of last resort is the customer._
> 
> _Let the customer's purchases — not the advertiser's opinions — be the test."_

**The Golden Rule:**

**Opinion (yours, the client's, "experts'") ≠ truth.**

**Testing = truth.**

**Sales = absolute truth.**

#### **IMPLICATION #4 — A Copywriter Must Understand Sales (Not Just Copy)**

Hopkins:

> _"The best advertising men are those who have had selling experience._
> 
> _They know customer reactions._
> 
> _They know how and where to strike._
> 
> _They know from experience what arguments are useless."_

**Historical validation (the masters prove it with their biographies):**

**John E. Kennedy:**

**Before copywriting (1880s-1890s):**

-   Door-to-door book salesman (Canadian Prairies)
-   Door-to-door Bible salesman (rural areas)
-   Top salesman (broke regional records)

**He learned:**

-   Real objections ("I don't have money," "I don't have time to read," "I already have a Bible")
-   What works face-to-face (reason-why: "Why THIS Bible is different...")
-   How to close (ask for the signature, don't assume)

**Afterward (1904):**

-   Applied his sales knowledge to copy
-   "Salesmanship in print" = a salesman's insight (not an advertiser's)

**Claude Hopkins:**

**Before copywriting:**

-   Door-to-door Bissell Carpet Sweeper salesman (1880s)
-   Top salesman (Midwest region)
-   Demonstrated the product (swept the carpet in front of the prospect)
-   Closed the sale (signature, scheduled delivery)

**He learned:**

-   Demonstration > claims (showing it works > saying it works)
-   Specific objections ("Too expensive" → "But it lasts 10 years, cost/day = $0.05")
-   Follow-up (a satisfied customer = referrals — Hopkins tracked this)

**Afterward (1900s-1920s):**

-   Became the highest-paid copywriter ($185,000/year in 1907 = ~$5.5M today)
-   Applied sales to copy (free samples = letting the prospect "try it" just as he did door-to-door)

**David Ogilvy:**

**Before advertising:**

-   Door-to-door AGA stove salesman (Scotland, 1935)
-   Rural area (isolated farms)
-   Demonstrated the stove (cooked a meal in the prospect's house)
-   Closed sales of $400+ (a fortune in 1935)

**He learned:**

-   Deep research (knew the product better than the engineers — read the full technical manual)
-   Handling skepticism (Scottish farmers = ultra-skeptical)
-   Factual selling (specific facts > hyperbole: "Stove maintains temperature ±2°C for 8 hours" vs. "the best stove in the world")

**Afterward (1948+):**

-   Founded Ogilvy & Mather
-   Applied factual selling to copy (Rolls-Royce: "At 60 mph, the loudest noise is the electric clock" — a specific fact, not a generic claim)

**Gary Halbert:**

**He didn't sell door-to-door, but:**

He studied sales obsessively:

> _"I handwrote sales letters from top closers — word for word — to absorb their patterns._
> 
> _I listened to hundreds of hours of sales calls (recorded)._
> 
> _I interviewed top salespeople: 'What objection kills most deals? How do you overcome it?'_
> 
> _Copy IS sales. If you don't understand sales, you don't understand copy."_

**A clear pattern across 4 masters:**

**They understood SALES before/during copywriting.**

**Copy = the application of sales knowledge to a print/digital medium.**

**Implication for you (the modern copywriter):**

**If you don't understand sales, invest time in it:**

**20% of your study time = sales** (not just copywriting).

**How to study sales:**

**A. Read sales books (not copy books):**

-   SPIN Selling (Rackham)
-   The Challenger Sale (Dixon, Adamson)
-   Never Split the Difference (Voss)
-   To Sell Is Human (Pink)

**B. Listen to real sales calls:**

-   Gong.io (library of recorded calls — search for your industry)
-   YouTube: "sales call recording" (filter by niche)
-   Podcasts: Sales calls deconstructed

**C. Observe top salespeople:**

-   How they qualify (specific questions)
-   How they diagnose (SPIN or similar)
-   How they handle objections (techniques, language)
-   How they close (ask explicitly, create urgency)

**D. Role-play (practice):**

-   Before writing copy, sell the product VERBALLY (record yourself or to a friend)
-   If you can sell it by talking: Transcribe it into copy (a solid base)
-   If you CAN'T sell it by talking: The problem is the OFFER or POSITIONING (not the copy) — go back to Docs 3-4

**Kennedy:**

> _"If you cannot sell a product face-to-face, you cannot sell it in print._
> 
> _Master sales first. Copy second."_

#### **IMPLICATION #5 — Traceability is Mandatory (Measure Everything or Die)**

**Kennedy introduced "keyed advertising" (1904):**

Before Kennedy: Ads without tracking (impossible to know which one worked).

**Kennedy's innovation:**

Each ad published = a unique code.

**Example:**

Ad in _Saturday Evening Post_: "Send in coupon #101"  
Ad in _Collier's Magazine_: "Send in coupon #102"  
Ad in _Life Magazine_: "Send in coupon #103"

The customer sends in the coupon → The sale is attributed to the specific publication.

**Result:** Knowing EXACTLY which ad sold and which one failed.

**Hopkins (1920s), obsessed with tracking:**

> _"Almost any question can be answered, cheaply, quickly and finally, by a test campaign._
> 
> _And that's the way to answer them — not by arguments around a table._
> 
> _Go to the court of last resort — the buyers of your product."_

And then:

> _"The advertising is keyed._
> 
> _We trace results to the fraction of a penny._
> 
> _We know the cost per customer to the penny._
> 
> _We know the probable cost of getting additional customers."_

**Real example (Hopkins — Schlitz Beer, 1904):**

3 ads, 3 publications:

| Publication | Coupon | Responses | Cost | Cost/Customer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sat. Eve. Post | #1 | 847 | $1,020 | $1.20 |
| Collier's | #2 | 412 | $990 | $2.40 |
| Life | #3 | 1,231 | $1,050 | $0.85 |

**The decision (data-driven):**

Scale ad #3 (Life — CAC $0.85, the best).

Cut ad #2 (Collier's — CAC $2.40, the worst).

Maintain #1 (Sat Eve Post — CAC $1.20, average).

**The result:**

ROI increased by 183% (vs. distributing the budget equally without measuring).

**Only because he MEASURED.**

**Modern (2024 — same principles, superior tools):**

**Tracking methods (digital):**

**UTM Parameters:**

```
product.com/offer?utm_source=facebook&amp;utm_medium=cpc&amp;utm_campaign=nov2024&amp;utm_content=headline-a
```

Tracks: Source (Facebook), Medium (CPC), Campaign (Nov), Content (Headline A).

Google Analytics separates conversions by UTM.

**You know:** Headline A (Facebook, Nov) generated X sales.

**Pixels:**

-   Facebook Pixel (tracks visitors, conversions, retargeting)
-   Google Analytics (behavior flow, conversions)
-   Custom events (video watched 50%, button clicked, etc)

**Unique Promo Codes:**

-   FACEBOOK10 (Facebook ads)
-   GOOGLE15 (Google ads)
-   EMAIL20 (email campaign)
-   LAUNCH50 (product launch)

At checkout: The customer enters the code → The sale is attributed to the source.

**Dedicated Landing Pages:**

-   product.com/fb-nov (Facebook Nov campaign)
-   product.com/google-q4 (Google Q4)
-   product.com/webinar-dec (December Webinar)

A different URL → the conversion is attributed to the campaign.

**Exclusive Phone Numbers:**

-   (555) 300-0001 (Facebook ads)
-   (555) 300-0002 (Google ads)
-   (555) 300-0003 (Email campaign)

Call tracking: Which number was called → the source is identified.

**QR Codes:**

-   Flyer A: QR code 1 (tracks scans)
-   Flyer B: QR code 2

Offline → Online (traceable).

**Every element is tracked:**

-   Which headline converted better? (A/B test: Headline A vs. B)
-   Which email in the sequence generated the most sales? (Email 5? Email 7? → optimize)
-   Which traffic source has the best LTV:CAC? (Facebook? Google? Organic? → allocate budget)
-   Which offer converted better? ($997 pay-in-full? 3×$397? → choose)
-   Which guarantee level? (30 days? 60? 90? → test)

**The law remains (Hopkins 1923 = truth in 2024):**

**"Test everything. Assume nothing. Measure or die."**

Hopkins:

> _"The time to learn the result of an advertisement is BEFORE you spend the money — not after._
> 
> _A test campaign answers questions for a few dollars which might otherwise cost thousands."_

**Modern translation:**

A/B test with $500 (100-200 people) → Identify the winner (headline A vs. B) → Scale with $50,000 (with confidence).

**vs.**

Spend 50,000withouttesting→Discoveritfailed→$$ lost, zero learned.

**Tracking = the difference between science and gambling.**

### **Scientific Validation (8 Decades Later):**

**Kahneman & Tversky (1979 — Prospect Theory, Nobel Prize 2002):**

They studied how **salespeople persuade prospects**.

They discovered psychological principles:

-   **Framing** (presenting as a gain vs. a loss dramatically alters the decision)
-   **Anchoring** (the first price mentioned = a permanent mental reference)
-   **Loss Aversion** (losing $100 hurts 2-2.5x more than gaining $100 pleases)
-   **Endowment Effect** (owning something increases its perceived value by 2-3x)

Then, they studied **advertising/copy** by applying the same principles.

**Critical discovery:**

**A personal salesman uses framing** (consciously or unconsciously).

**Copy uses framing** (consciously or unconsciously).

**The SAME psychological mechanisms.** Exactly.

A test (Kahneman):

A prospect chooses between plan A vs. B.

The salesman presents: "Plan A saves you $500/year" (gain frame).

vs.

The salesman presents: "Without Plan A, you LOSE $500/year" (loss frame).

**Same information. Opposite frames. Different decisions.**

**Copy does EXACTLY the same thing** (framing gain vs. loss).

**Cialdini (1984 — Influence):**

He infiltrated **personal sales** organizations AND **advertising agencies**.

He discovered **7 principles** (Part II of this document).

He validated:

**Personal sales use:**

-   Reciprocity (free samples in door-to-door sales)
-   Authority (salesman shows credentials)
-   Social Proof ("500 of your neighbors have already bought this")
-   Scarcity ("Offer expires today")
-   Commitment (progressive micro-yeses)
-   Liking (rapport building)
-   Unity ("We" from the same community)

**Advertising/Copy use:**

**THE SAME 7** (exactly).

**Scientific conclusion (Kahneman + Cialdini, 1979-1984):**

**Kennedy was right (1904).**

**Advertising IS salesmanship** (not an analogy, not a metaphor — it's an IDENTITY).

**Same essence, different medium.**

║                                                    ║

║ "Advertising is salesmanship in print"            ║
║                                                    ║
║ EXPANSION (Hopkins, 1923):                         ║
║ "The only purpose is to sell"                     ║
║                                                    ║
║ THE 5 CRITICAL IMPLICATIONS:                       ║
║                                                    ║
║ 1. COPY = A MULTIPLIED SALESMAN                   ║
║    • Salesman: 10 prospects/day                   ║
║    • Copy: 10,000 prospects/day                   ║
║    • Same CVR (33%), 1,000x the reach             ║
║                                                    ║
║ 2. SAME STRUCTURE (6 Identical Steps)             ║
║    Sales: Qualify → Diagnose → Present            ║
║           → Prove → Objections → Close            ║
║    Copy: Headline → Lead → Body → Proof → FAQ     ║
║          → CTA                                     ║
║    (Identical structure, different medium)        ║
║                                                    ║
║ 3. THE METRIC = SALES (The Only, Absolute One)    ║
║    Hopkins/Ogilvy/Halbert: "If it doesn't sell,   ║
║    it isn't creative"                              ║
║    Not: Awards, opinions, buzz                    ║
║    Yes: $, CVR%, CAC, LTV, ROI                    ║
║                                                    ║
║ 4. COPYWRITER ← SALESMAN (Base Knowledge)         ║
║    Masters: Kennedy, Hopkins, Ogilvy — all        ║
║    sold BEFORE writing copy                       ║
║    You: Study sales (20% of your time on sales)   ║
║                                                    ║
║ 5. TRACEABILITY IS MANDATORY (Measure Everything) ║
║    Kennedy 1904: Keyed coupons                    ║
║    Today 2024: UTMs, pixels, promo codes          ║
║    Hopkins: "Test BEFORE you spend"               ║
║                                                    ║
║ SCIENTIFIC VALIDATION (80 years later):            ║
║ • Kahneman (1979): Sales and copy = the same      ║
║   principles (Framing, Anchoring, Loss Aversion)  ║
║ • Cialdini (1984): 7 Principles in sales AND copy ║
║   (Reciprocity, Authority, Social Proof, etc)     ║
║                                                    ║
║ DEFINITIVE QUOTE:                                  ║
║ "Copy that doesn't sell isn't copy. It's art in the ║
║ wrong place." — A synthesis of Hopkins/Ogilvy/Halbert ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
```

║   (Specific testimonials, data, guarantee)        ║
║                                                    ║

║   (FAQ pre-empts and handles the top 5)           ║
║                                                    ║

║   (CTA is specific, not a vague "learn more")     ║
║                                                    ║

║                                                    ║
║ • 5-6/6 ✅ Copy = a good salesman (keep, launch)  ║
║ • 3-4/6 ⚠️ Copy = an average salesman (improve)    ║
║ • &lt;3/6 ❌ Copy = a weak salesman (rewrite or      ║
║   fire — don't use it)                            ║

║ SCORE: 0/6 (this salesman would be fired on day one)║

║ Copy result: Confusion. The prospect doesn't know what║
║ is being sold, for whom, or why they should choose it.║
║ Bounce rate: 85%+. Conversion: ~0%.               ║
║                                                    ║
║ The problem: ZERO sales structure (just buzzwords).║
║                                                    ║
║ ═══════════════════════════════════════════════   ║
║                                                    ║
║ EXPECTED TEST (Validation):                       ║
║                                                    ║
║ Copy structured like sales (6 steps) vs.          ║
║ copy without structure (buzzwords, vague)         ║
║                                                    ║
║ Result (Hopkins/Ogilvy/Schwartz/Agora/Empiricus   ║
║ all tested this, and converge):                   ║
║                                                    ║
║ Structured: +40-100% conversion                   ║
║ (sometimes +200-500% when the unstructured is very║
║ bad)                                               ║
║                                                    ║
║ A sales structure ALWAYS beats chaos.             ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
```

## **SECTION 1.2 — AXIOM 2: COPY DOESN'T CREATE DESIRE — IT CHANNELS PRE-EXISTING DESIRE**

### **THE DISCOVERY (Schwartz, 1966):**

**Eugene M. Schwartz**, after 20 years of writing copy for health products (Rodale Press), investments (financial newsletters), and self-help (correspondence courses), distilled his greatest discovery in _Breakthrough Advertising_ (1966).

**Page 1 (the book's opening):**

> _"Your market is a mob._
> 
> _A great, blind, wild mob._
> 
> _Roaring with desire._
> 
> _Your job is not to create that desire._
> 
> _Your job is to direct it — to channel it — to focus it on your particular product."_

And then, page 18 (the central revelation):

> _"This is the copywriter's task:_
> 
> _Not to CREATE this mass desire —_
> 
> _But to CHANNEL and DIRECT it."_

**A conceptual revolution (destroying a common illusion):**

Until Schwartz (pre-1966), most copywriters thought:

"My job is to CONVINCE people to want X."

"I will CREATE desire with brilliant copy, a perfect argument, and irresistible persuasion."

"People don't want it yet, but I will make them want it."

**Schwartz destroyed this fantasy:**

"**You CANNOT create desire.**

Desire either already exists (in the market, in the hearts and minds of millions).

**OR** desire does not exist (and never will, no matter your copy).

**Your real job:**

1.  **Identify WHERE** intense desire ALREADY exists (which market/segment)
2.  **Direct** that desire toward YOUR product (vs. a competitor or vs. inertia/procrastination)
3.  **Amplify** the existing desire (make it conscious if it's latent, make it urgent if it's vague)

**You CANNOT:**

❌ Make a sedentary person (who HATES exercise) desire to work out  
❌ Make a person happy in their corporate job (loves security/benefits) desire risky entrepreneurship  
❌ Make a person uninterested in money (their values are different — art, spirituality) desire material wealth  
❌ Make a person without a specific pain desire your specific solution

**Desire is a PRE-CONDITION** (it exists before you, independent of you).

**Copy works WHERE desire exists.**

**Copy fails WHERE desire does not exist** (no matter how brilliant the copy is).

Schwartz:

> _"Copy is not some magic formula that creates desire where none exists._
> 
> _Copy is simply a tool to channel existing desire toward your product._
> 
> _If desire doesn't exist, copy is impotent."_

### **THE DEFINITIVE ANALOGY (Schwartz):**

**A copywriter is NOT:**

❌ A **magician** (pulling a rabbit out of an empty hat — creating something from nothing)

❌ A **divine creator** (generating desire ex nihilo, from a vacuum)

**A copywriter IS:**

✅ A **hydraulic engineer** (or water engineer)

**The engineer's job:**

**STEP 1:** Find a RIVER with a strong flow (running water, high volume, constant speed).

**STEP 2:** Build a CHANNEL (a dam, an aqueduct, a pipeline).

**STEP 3:** Direct the river's water to a MILL (the water wheel turns, grains are ground, flour is produced).

**The engineer does NOT create the river** (impossible — it requires rain, springs, gravity, geography).

**The river already exists** (natural forces).

**The engineer only CHANNELS it** (directs the existing flow for a productive use).

**If an engineer tries to "create a river" where there's no water:**

They build a channel in the desert. The mill is ready. **Zero water.**

**Total failure** (no matter how well-built the channel is).

**Copywriting is IDENTICAL:**

**The river = DESIRE** (it already exists in the market, or it doesn't).

**The channel = COPY** (you build it to direct the desire).

**The mill = THE PRODUCT** (it receives the channeled desire, converts it into sales).

**Copy channels pre-existing desire to the product.**

**Copy does not create desire from scratch** (impossible — desire is the "river," you don't control the rain/springs).

Schwartz:

> _"The power, the force, the overwhelming urge to own that makes advertising work, comes from the MARKET ITSELF — not from the copy._
> 
> _Copy cannot create desire for a product._
> 
> _It can only take the hopes, dreams, fears and desires that already exist in the hearts of millions of people, and focus those already-existing desires onto a particular product._
> 
> _This is the copywriter's task."_

### **WHY THIS IS TRUE (3 Irrefutable Reasons):**

#### **REASON #1 — Desires are Biological/Cultural (Not Created by Advertising)**

**Life-Force 8** (Whitman — Section 7.0 of this document):

8 biological impulses hardwired into the human brain (via evolution — 200,000+ years of Homo sapiens):

1.  **Survival** (self-preservation)
2.  **Enjoyment of Food & Beverages**
3.  **Freedom from Fear, Pain, and Danger**
4.  **Sexual Companionship** (reproduction, attraction, romance)
5.  **Comfortable Living Conditions** (physical comfort, money, convenience)
6.  **To Be Superior** (to compete, to win, status, hierarchy)
7.  **Care and Protection of Loved Ones** (protecting family, providing for children)
8.  **Social Approval** (to be accepted, admired, respected by the group)

**These 8 have existed for:**

200,000+ years (the evolution of Homo sapiens)

**Some (food, reproduction, survival) have existed for:**

Millions of years (all animal life has these drives).

**Advertising has existed for:**

120 years (1900s-2024).

**Advertising did NOT CREATE:**

❌ The desire for food (LF2 — hunger has existed for millions of years, a fundamental biological drive)

❌ The desire for sex/companionship (LF4 — reproduction; the species wouldn't exist without it)

❌ The desire for safety/survival (LF1, LF3 — the fear of death/pain is ancestral)

❌ The desire for status/superiority (LF6, LF8 — primates have social hierarchies, dominance)

❌ The desire for comfort/money (LF5 — resources = survival, comfort-seeking is universal)

❌ The desire to protect one's family (LF7 — the parental instinct, millions of years old)

**Advertising only:**

✅ **CONNECTS products to pre-existing desires**

**Examples:**

**Perfume:**

-   Does NOT create: The desire for sexual attraction (LF4 — has existed for millions of years)
-   It connects: Perfume → attraction (smell = an evolutionary signal)

**Home alarm system:**

-   Does NOT create: The fear of invasion/danger (LF3 — an ancestral fear)
-   It connects: Alarm system → safety (a solution for a pre-existing fear)

**A copywriting course:**

-   Does NOT create: The desire for money/comfort (LF5 — universal, intense)
-   It connects: Copywriting (a skill) → money/freedom (a pre-existing desire)

**Copy articulates the connection between product → desire.**

**Copy does NOT generate the base desire** (it's biological/cultural, not created by advertising).

#### **REASON #2 — Schwartz Tested This Empirically (40 Years, Thousands of Promos)**

**Schwartz's career (1946-1986):**

40 years of writing copy.

Thousands of promotions (newsletters, courses, health products).

Revenue generated: $100M+ (estimated, directly from his promos).

**Schwartz tried (experimentally) to sell products that NO ONE wanted:**

Obscure products, tiny niches, without validated market desire.

**Result:** Total failure.

"Brilliant" copy couldn't save them.

"Brilliant" headlines didn't work.

"Unique" mechanisms didn't matter.

"Strong" guarantees didn't help.

**Non-existent desire = impotent copy** (no matter the skill of the copywriter).

**Then, Schwartz focused ONLY on products that markets ALREADY desired intensely:**

-   Financial newsletters (pre-existing desire: money, financial security — LF5, universal)
-   Health products (desire: vitality, longevity, avoiding pain — LF1, LF3, intense)
-   Self-help courses (desire: success, status, respect — LF6, LF8, strong)

**Result:** Massive success.

**Mediocre** copy sold well (because the market desire was intense — Schwartz just channeled it).

**Schwartz's conclusion (40 years distilled):**

> _"I can write copy that channels existing intense desire and generate millions in sales._
> 
> _I CANNOT write copy that creates desire where none exists and make a single dime._
> 
> _This is not modesty._
> 
> _This is a LAW — observed empirically over 40 years and thousands of campaigns."_

**Temporal validation:**

1946-1966 (20 years): Schwartz tests creating vs. channeling

1966: Publishes his discovery (_Breakthrough Advertising_)

1966-1986 (20 years): Applies his discovery (only channels, never tries to create)

**Revenue explodes** (his most profitable decades).

**This is not theory.**

It is an **observed law** (from decades of market experiments, millions in testing).

#### **REASON #3 — Modern Psychology Validates This Scientifically (Kahneman, Ariely)**

**Daniel Kahneman** (_Thinking, Fast and Slow_, 2011):

Humans have **pre-formed preferences** (System 1 — automatic, emotional).

System 2 (rational) can **modify** preferences slightly.

**But:** System 2 is lazy (avoids effort).

**Result:** Most decisions = System 1 (automatic preferences, not deeply thought out).

**Implication for copy:**

**Copy can:**

-   ✅ Activate a pre-existing preference (System 1 responds automatically)
-   ✅ Make a latent preference conscious (bring it to the surface)
-   ✅ Amplify a weak preference (make it strong via context, urgency)

**Copy CANNOT:**

-   ❌ Create a completely new preference (would require extensive System 2 — deep analysis, a change of values — which people intensely resist)

**Dan Ariely** (_Predictably Irrational_, 2008):

Desires are **relative** (in comparison to alternatives), not absolute.

**Copy can:**

-   ✅ Make a product seem MORE desirable (via context, decoy pricing, framing)
-   ✅ Make a benefit more salient (amplify a specific aspect)

**But:** The BASE desire for that **type of thing** must exist.

**Example (Chocolate Experiment — Ariely):**

Lindt (premium) vs. Hershey's Kiss.

Pricing was manipulated:

-   Scenario A: Lindt $0.15, Kiss $0.01 → 73% chose Lindt
-   Scenario B: Lindt $0.14, Kiss $0.00 → 69% chose Kiss

**"Free" reversed the preferences** (Hershey's won when it was $0).

**BUT (this is critical):**

The experiment **ASSUMES the desire for chocolate already exists**.

The participants already WANT chocolate (sweetness, pleasure — LF2).

**If a participant HATES chocolate** (allergy, diet, personal preference):

Neither a free Lindt nor a free Kiss works.

**Non-existent base desire = impotent copy/pricing.**

**Ariely can:**

-   ✅ Change WHICH chocolate (Lindt vs. Kiss — a relative preference)
-   ✅ AMPLIFY the desire for chocolate (via "free" — zero risk)

**Ariely CANNOT:**

-   ❌ Create a desire for chocolate in someone who doesn't want sweets

**Scientific conclusion (Kahneman + Ariely converge with Schwartz):**

**Copy can amplify, direct, and make salient an existing desire.**

**Copy cannot generate a non-existent desire.**

**Schwartz was right** (science validated it 40+ years later).

### **MASS × INTENSITY — The Market Value Formula**

**Schwartz** (_Breakthrough Advertising_, Chapter 1, pages 19-24):

Not all markets are equal.

**Market value = a function of 2 variables:**

```
MARKET VALUE = Mass (size) × Intensity (strength of desire)
```

**MASS (Market Size):**

How many people have the desire/problem?

-   100 people = very low mass
-   10,000 people = low mass
-   100,000 people = medium mass
-   1,000,000 people = high mass
-   10,000,000+ people = very high mass

**INTENSITY (Strength of Desire — Scale of 1-10):**

How strong is the desire? How much does it hurt? What would they pay? Do they lose sleep over it?

**1-3 (Low):**

-   Vague, casual desire, "nice to have"
-   Not urgent, not painful
-   Would pay very little (<$10)
-   Example: "It would be nice to be more organized"

**4-6 (Medium):**

-   Moderate desire, a "want"
-   Some annoyance, but tolerable
-   Would pay a moderate amount ($50-200)
-   Example: "I want to lose 10 lbs" (slightly overweight)

**7-9 (High):**

-   Intense desire, a "need"
-   Acute pain or a strong craving
-   Would pay a high amount ($500-5,000)
-   They lose sleep, it affects their health/relationships
-   Example: "I need to lose 60 lbs" (obesity, risk of diabetes)

**10 (Extreme):**

-   Unbearable pain or an obsessive desire
-   Maximum urgency, desperation
-   Would pay ANYTHING ($10k+)
-   Example: "My child has cancer, I need a cure" (life-or-death)

### **THE 4 QUADRANTS (Combinations of Mass × Intensity):**

**QUADRANT A — HIGH Mass × HIGH Intensity:**

**Example:** "To make money" (billions of people × a strong, universal desire)

**Characteristics:**

-   HUGE market (10M+ people)
-   INTENSE desire (8-10 on the scale)
-   Market Value Score: Very high (10M × 8 = 80M)

**Advantage:** Astronomical revenue potential (giant market + willing to pay)

**Disadvantage:** FIERCE competition (all copywriters want this market)

**Viability:** Difficult (unless you have a revolutionary Big Idea — Doc 3, or a sustainable unique advantage)

**QUADRANT B — HIGH Mass × LOW Intensity:**

**Example:** "To be more organized" (billions might be interested × a vague, casual desire)

**Characteristics:**

-   Large market (10M+ people)
-   WEAK desire (2-4 on the scale)
-   Market Value Score: Medium-low (10M × 3 = 30M)

**Problem:** The desire is insufficient to motivate a PURCHASE.

People say "yes, I want that" (in a survey), but they don't ACT (they don't pay, they don't implement).

**Result:** Very low conversion (0.1-0.5% is typical).

**Viability:** POOR (avoid — a lot of effort for little return).

**QUADRANT C — LOW Mass × HIGH Intensity:** ⭐ **THE SWEET SPOT**

**Example:** "To cure severe cystic acne in competitive teenagers applying to college (who need photos for applications with a close deadline)"

**Characteristics:**

-   SMALL market (hundreds of thousands, not billions — a niche)
-   EXTREME desire (9-10 on the scale — acute social pain, a real deadline, critical self-image)
-   Market Value Score: High (500k × 9 = 4.5M)

**Advantages:**

-   Less competition (niche — most ignore small markets)
-   VERY HIGH conversion (acute pain = extreme willingness to pay, 5-15% CVR is possible)
-   Premium pricing (a 9-10 pain = they would pay $500-2,000 for a solution)
-   High customer lifetime value (problem solved = gratitude, loyalty, referrals)
-   Organic word-of-mouth (niches have tight networks)

**Viability:** **EXCELLENT** (a profitable niche — a favorite of Schwartz).

**QUADRANT D — LOW Mass × LOW Intensity:**

**Example:** "To collect stamps from Bulgaria issued between 1952-1957"

**Characteristics:**

-   TINY market (hundreds, maybe thousands globally)
-   WEAK desire (2-3 — a casual, non-urgent hobby)
-   Market Value Score: Terrible (1,000 × 2 = 2,000)

**Problem:** The market is too small + the desire is too weak = impossible to scale.

**Viability:** TERRIBLE (avoid completely — not worth the effort).

### **The Distilled Wisdom (Schwartz — Prioritization):**

> _"I would rather have a market of 10,000 people with INTENSE desire (a 9-10 pain)_
> 
> _Than a market of 10 million people with vague interest (a 2-3 pain)."_

**Why Quadrant C > Quadrant B?**

**The math (a real example):**

**Market A (Quadrant B — High Mass, Low Intensity):**

-   Size: 10,000,000 people
-   Intensity: 3 (vague)
-   CVR: 0.1% (weak desire = few buy)
-   Price: $10 (commoditization, price war)
-   Revenue: 10M × 0.1% × $10 = **$100,000**

**Problems:**

-   Extreme competition (commoditization)
-   Low pricing (can't charge more)
-   High churn (low commitment)
-   High CAC (requires massive ads to stand out in a saturated market)

**Market B (Quadrant C — Low Mass, High Intensity):**

-   Size: 10,000 people
-   Intensity: 9 (acute pain)
-   CVR: 10% (intense desire = high conversion)
-   Price: $100 (premium — acute pain = willingness to pay)
-   Revenue: 10k × 10% × $100 = **$100,000**

**SAME revenue, BUT:**

-   Low competition (niche is ignored by the masses)
-   Premium pricing (defensible via specialization)
-   Low churn (problem solved = gratitude)
-   Low CAC (word-of-mouth, precise targeting)

**Market B is superior** (same revenue, better economics, a sustainable business).

Schwartz:

> _"Intensity is more important than Mass._
> 
> _10,000 people in agony will make you rich._
> 
> _10 million people mildly interested will make you poor."_

**Prioritize Intensity over Mass** (whenever possible).

**A Starving Crowd** (Halbert — next section) = Intensity 8-10 (not necessarily Mass).

### **CRITICAL IMPLICATIONS FOR COPYWRITING:**

#### **IMPLICATION #1 — Market Selection > Brilliant Copy (The 80/20 Law)**

**Gary Halbert** (_The Boron Letters_, Letter #2, validating Schwartz):

> _"The best copy in the world, written by the most brilliant copywriter, cannot sell ice to Eskimos._
> 
> _It cannot sell sand to a Bedouin in the desert._
> 
> _It cannot sell what NO ONE WANTS."_

And the classic analogy (the hamburger stand):

> _"Imagine: You're going to open a hamburger stand._
> 
> _I give you only ONE competitive advantage (just one, choose wisely)._
> 
> _What would you choose?_
> 
> _• The best meat? (Wagyu beef, premium quality)_
> 
> _• The best location? (A busy corner, high foot traffic)_
> 
> _• The best cook? (A Michelin-star chef, perfect burgers)_
> 
> _• The best price? (Cheaper than all competitors)_
> 
> _• The best service? (Trained staff, 5-star service)_
> 
> _• The best marketing? (Brilliant ads, superior branding)_
> 
> **_THE RIGHT ANSWER:_**
> 
> **_A STARVING CROWD._**
> 
> _People with INTENSE HUNGER > everything else._
> 
> _A mediocre hamburger (OK meat, OK location, OK price) + starving people = A LINE OUT THE DOOR._
> 
> _A PERFECT hamburger (Wagyu, Michelin chef, prime location) + people who AREN'T HUNGRY = BANKRUPTCY._
> 
> _Hunger (intense desire) decides everything."_

**The brutal translation for copywriting:**

**80% of your success = choosing a market with acute pain/intense desire** (A Starving Crowd, Intensity 8-10)

**20% = copy that communicates the solution** (well-executed, clear, persuasive)

**Choose the WRONG market** (Intensity <5, vague/non-existent desire):

→ Brilliant copy fails (tries to create a river where there's no water — Schwartz's analogy)

→ Brilliant headlines don't save it (there's no desire to channel)

→ Irresistible offers don't convert (no one wants it, even for free)

**Choose the RIGHT market** (Intensity 8-10, validated acute pain):

→ **Mediocre** copy wins (the desire already exists, copy just articulates the obvious solution)

→ Simple headlines work ("Cure \[intense pain\]" — straight to the point)

→ Basic offers convert (a serious problem = they are willing to pay)

**Halbert (hierarchy of importance):**

```
SUCCESS = (List Quality × Offer × Copy) / Competition

List Quality (The Starving Crowd) = 50% of the result
The Offer (Irresistible) = 25%
The Copy (Well-executed) = 25%
```

**The list (the market with desire) is HALF of your success.**

**The copy is only a quarter.**

**The shocking implication:**

**Brilliant copy (top 1%) for a bad market (Intensity 3) < Mediocre copy (top 50%) for an excellent market (Intensity 9).**

**Schwartz/Halbert (consensus):**

> _"Choose your market wisely. Copy cannot overcome market indifference."_

#### **IMPLICATION #2 — Deep Research BEFORE Copy (Not After, Not During)**

Schwartz:

> _"The power, the force, the overwhelming urge to own that makes advertising work, comes from the MARKET ITSELF — not from the copy._
> 
> _Therefore: Study the market BEFORE you write a single word."_

**BEFORE you write copy:**

You MUST know (→ REQUIREMENT: a complete Doc 2 = answering these 5 mandatory questions):

**QUESTION #1 — WHERE is the desire?**

Which specific market/niche/segment has an intense pain or desire?

**Not enough:** "Everyone wants X" (vague, not targetable, not actionable)

**Sufficient:** "Brazilian digital entrepreneurs, 35-55, making R$10-50k/month for 12+ months (a plateau), frustrated with slow growth, considering giving up or completely changing their business"

**Specific = targetable** (you know where to find them via Facebook groups, Instagram hashtags, forums, events).

**QUESTION #2 — HOW intense is the desire?**

A 1-10 scale (Intensity).

**How to find out:**

**Method A — How much have they spent trying to solve it?**

-   $0-100: Intensity 2-4 (casual)
-   $100-500: Intensity 5-6 (moderate)
-   $500-2,000: Intensity 7-8 (strong)
-   $2,000-10,000: Intensity 9 (extreme)
-   $10,000+: Intensity 10 (desperation)

**Method B — Do they lose sleep over it?**

-   No: Intensity ≤6
-   Occasionally: Intensity 7
-   Frequently: Intensity 8-9
-   Every night: Intensity 10

**Method C — Does it affect their health/relationships?**

-   No: Intensity ≤5
-   Slightly: Intensity 6-7
-   Significantly: Intensity 8-9
-   Destroying their life: Intensity 10

**Method D — How much would they be willing to pay for a DEFINITIVE solution?**

-   <$100: Intensity ≤5
-   $100-500: Intensity 6-7
-   $500-2,000: Intensity 8
-   $2,000-10,000: Intensity 9
-   $10,000+: Intensity 10
-   "Any price" / "Everything I have": Intensity 10

**Target:** Intensity 7+ (minimum).

**Ideal:** Intensity 8-10.

**If <7:** Consider a different market (insufficient desire to motivate action/payment).

**QUESTION #3 — WHAT language do they use to describe their pain/desire?**

The EXACT words the avatar uses (not your words, not professional jargon).

**Not enough:** Your words ("optimizing the operational flow of recurring revenue")

**Sufficient:** Their words ("I'm SCREWED. I work like a dog and I'm not getting anywhere. I'm sick of it. I need to get out of this crap.")

**How to find the exact language:**

**A. Mine Amazon reviews:**

-   A product similar to yours (or a competitor's)
-   1- to 3-star reviews (the dissatisfied — the language of pain)
-   5-star reviews (the satisfied — the language of achieved desire)
-   Ctrl+F for emotional words: "frustrated," "finally," "changed my life," "disappointed"
-   Copy the exact phrases (they describe the problem/solution better than you can)

**B. Lurk in forums/groups:**

-   Reddit (r/entrepreneur, r/fitness, r/personalfinance, etc — search for your niche)
-   Facebook Groups (for your avatar)
-   Quora (questions about the problem)
-   Observe: How do they describe their pain? What words do they repeat? What's the emotional tone?

**C. Interview 5-10 people from your avatar group:**

-   Open-ended questions: "What's your biggest frustration with \[topic\]?" "How would you describe the problem?" "What have you tried?"
-   **RECORD** (with permission) and **TRANSCRIBE**
-   The raw (unedited) language = pure gold

**Collier** (_The Robert Collier Letter Book_, 1931):

> _"Enter the conversation already happening in the reader's mind."_

**The conversation = the language they ALREADY USE** (not what you want them to use, not what sounds "professional").

**In your copy:**

**Use THEIR words** (transcribed from research).

**Do NOT translate** it into "better" language (polished, professional, edited).

**Raw = authentic** (the prospect thinks: "They speak my language" → instant identification → Cialdini's Liking/Similarity).

**QUESTION #4 — What have they ALREADY TRIED (and why did it fail)?**

A list of previous solutions that did NOT work.

**Why this matters (dual value):**

**Value A — Identifying future objections:**

Each failed attempt = an **objection** that will come up:

"I've already tried X, it didn't work." (skepticism based on experience)

**You MUST address this** (Doc 11 — handling the objection: "X failed because \[reason\], Y is different because \[differentiation\]").

**Value B — An opportunity to justify (Warren — One Sentence #2):**

Each failure = a chance to **externalize the blame** (protect their ego).

"It wasn't YOUR fault that X failed. X was a flawed system (or an inferior product, or wrong information). You were a VICTIM."

**Justifying their failures** → Removes shame/guilt → Mental openness to a new solution.

**How to find out:**

Interviews: "What have you already tried to solve \[problem\]?" "Why didn't it work?"

Reviews: Look for "disappointed," "didn't work," "waste of money" (fail stories).

**In your copy:**

"You tried a low-carb diet (and gained the weight back).

You tried keto (unsustainable, you quit in week 3).

You tried intermittent fasting (unbearable hunger, irritability).

**It wasn't your fault.**

These diets SHUT DOWN your metabolism (recent science proves it — the body enters starvation mode and burns even FEWER calories).

You were doing the right thing, but with the wrong information (not your fault)."

**Justifies 3 failures** → Prospect is relieved (I'm not a failure, the systems were flawed) → Open to a new solution.

**QUESTION #5 — What is the MASS × INTENSITY score of this market?**

A rough calculation (an estimate):

**Mass:** How many people (an estimate based on research — size of Facebook groups, Google Keyword Planner volume, industry reports)

**Intensity:** A score of 1-10 (based on Methods A-D above)

**Score:** Mass × Intensity

**Example:**

Market: "Brazilian digital entrepreneurs stuck at R$10-50k/month"

Mass: ~100,000 (estimate — FB groups combined, Google searches, industry)

Intensity: 8 (they've spent R$5k+ trying, they lose sleep, it affects their family, willing to pay R$2-10k for a solution)

**Score:** 100,000 × 8 = **800,000**

**Benchmarks:**

-   Score >1,000,000 = Excellent (pursue)
-   Score 100,000-1M = Good (viable)
-   Score 10,000-100k = Questionable (only if Intensity is 9-10)
-   Score <10,000 = Poor (avoid or reconsider)

**If Mass AND Intensity are both low (<10k Mass, <5 Intensity):**

→ **Choose a different market** (don't force it — success is unlikely).

**Compare potential markets:**

List 3-5 markets (different products/niches).

Calculate the score for each.

**Choose the one with the highest Intensity** (if the Mass is sufficient, >10k).

**OR the highest total score** (if multiple have similar Intensity).

### **Practical Implications (How to Apply Axiom 2):**

#### **APPLICATION #1 — Never Write Copy Before Validating Desire**

**The correct process (order matters):**

**STEP 1:** Identify a potential market (a rough avatar)

**STEP 2:** Do deep research (→ REQUIREMENT: a complete Doc 2 — the 5 questions above, 3-5 hours)

**STEP 3:** Validate that the desire exists (Intensity 7+, sufficient Mass)

**STEP 4:** **ONLY THEN:** Write the copy (channeling the validated desire)

**The WRONG process (common, fatal):**

**STEP 1:** I have a product

**STEP 2:** I write the copy (assuming desire exists)

**STEP 3:** I launch

**STEP 4:** Failure (desire didn't exist — you found out too late, money and time wasted)

**Schwartz:**

> _"More money is wasted on great copy for the wrong market than on poor copy for the right market."_

**The investment:**

3-5 hours of research (Doc 2) = **prevents** 20-40 hours of writing masterful copy for a market that doesn't want it.

**The ROI of research:** 4-8x (in time) + infinite (in money not wasted).

#### **APPLICATION #2 — The Product Must Connect to a REAL Desire (Not an Invented One)**

**The framework:**

**NOT:**

Product X → Invent a desire for X → Write copy to convince

**YES:**

Desire Y (pre-existing, validated) → Product X solves Y → Copy articulates the obvious connection

**Real Example (A Copywriting Course):**

**THE WRONG APPROACH (Violating Axiom 2):**

"I'm going to create a desire to 'learn copywriting' with amazing copy!"

**The problem:**

Very few people wake up thinking "Today I want to learn copywriting."

"Copywriting" is a technical word, with no emotional charge for 99% of the population.

**The desire for the word "copywriting" = non-existent** (low Mass, low Intensity).

**Result:** The copy fails (it tries to create desire from scratch — impossible).

**THE CORRECT APPROACH (Obeying Axiom 2):**

**STEP 1 — Identify a pre-existing desire (intense, universal):**

"Where are the people who want:\*\*

-   **MONEY** (R$10-30k/month, not R$3k)
-   **FREEDOM** (to work from home, a flexible schedule, no boss)
-   **AUTONOMY** (not depending on a job — fear of being fired)
-   **RESPECT** (to be an expert, family is proud, not 'just another person')"

**These desires (money, freedom, autonomy, respect) are UNIVERSAL, INTENSE** (LF5, LF6, LF8).

**The answer:** Entrepreneurs, freelancers, people unhappy in their jobs, mothers who want to stay with their children but need an income, retirees who need extra income.

**Mass:** Millions (in Brazil alone = 5-10M potential)

**Intensity:** 7-9 (money is a need, freedom is a strong dream)

**The desire (for money, freedom, autonomy) ALREADY EXISTS** (pre-validated, not invented).

**STEP 2 — Connect the product (copywriting) to the pre-existing desire:**

"Copywriting is a high-demand, low-supply skill that allows you to earn R$5-30k/month working from home (remote, freelance, flexible schedule)."

**Copywriting (the product/vehicle) is connected to:**

-   Money (R$5-30k/month — LF5)
-   Freedom (home, flexible — LF5)
-   Autonomy (freelance, no boss — LF6)
-   Respect (a rare skill, an expert — LF8)

**The connection is obvious, logical** (copywriting IS a means to achieve pre-existing desires).

**STEP 3 — The copy articulates the connection (it doesn't try to create desire):**

**The resulting headline:**

"How to Earn R$10-30k/Month Working From Home (Even With No Experience, Without Selling Face-to-Face, Without Relying on a Boss) Using This High-Demand Skill"

**Analysis:**

✅ It speaks to PRE-EXISTING DESIRES (R$10-30k, from home, no experience, no selling, no boss — validated in research, Intensity 8)

✅ It removes objections inline ("even with no experience" — a low barrier)

✅ It appeals to LF5 (money/comfort) + LF6 (autonomy) + LF8 (high-demand = status)

✅ "Skill" is mentioned (copywriting is implied), but it's NOT the focus

**The focus is the RESULT** (the achieved desires), not the product/means.

**The body copy later reveals:**

"The skill is copywriting. Here's how it works: You write copy for clients (emails, sales pages, ads) → You charge R$3-15k/project → 2-4 projects/month = R$6-60k. It's remote (Zoom calls), flexible (you choose clients/schedule), and you have no boss (you ARE the expert)."

**Copywriting (the vehicle) is revealed AFTER capturing them via desire.**

**The copy channeled:**

A pre-existing desire (for money, freedom, autonomy — validated, intense) → to the Vehicle (copywriting — the means to achieve those desires)

**The copy did NOT try to:**

❌ Create a desire for "copywriting" from scratch (impossible — the word has no emotional charge)

#### **APPLICATION #3 — The Drill Analogy (Ladeira — 5 Levels of Depth)**

**Theodore Levitt** (Harvard, 1960s — a marketing classic):

> _"People don't want a quarter-inch drill._
> 
> _They want a quarter-inch HOLE."_

**Ladeira (VTSD) expanded on this:**

"People don't want a hole. They want a HUNG PICTURE. They want a BEAUTIFUL HOME. They want to be ADMIRED for their good taste."

**The 5 levels (drilling down from the feature to identity):**

**Level 1 — FEATURE (The Drill):**

"A course with 20 modules, 40 hours of video, 15 templates, 200 swipes"

**No one DESIRES** "20 modules" (an abstract feature, no emotion).

**Level 2 — FUNCTIONAL BENEFIT (The Hole):**

"Learn to write copy that converts visitors into buyers"

**Better, but still rational** (moderate desire, Intensity 5-6).

**Level 3 — EMOTIONAL BENEFIT (The Hung Picture):**

"The freedom to sell without having to sell face-to-face (no cold calls, no uncomfortable pitching, no personal rejection)"

**Emotional** (freedom, avoiding pain — Intensity 7).

**Level 4 — ASPIRATIONAL (The Beautiful Home):**

"R$20k/month working 6 hours/day from home (time with family, travel, no commute, no boss)"

**The desired lifestyle** (a complete transformation — Intensity 8-9).

**Level 5 — IDENTITY (Pride/Status — Who You BECOME):**

"Become 'The Expert' your family admires, friends consult, and community respects. You HAVE authority, valuable knowledge, and complete independence."

**Identity/status** (LF6, LF8 — Intensity 9-10).

**Copy focuses on Levels 4-5** (aspirational/identity — the deepest, most intense desires).

**Not Levels 1-2** (feature/functional — no one DESIRES features, they desire TRANSFORMATION).

**The Mechanism (Levels 1-2) connects to the Transformation (Levels 4-5):**

"20 modules, templates, swipes (Level 1) → Write copy (Level 2) → Sell without calls (Level 3) → R$20k, 6h/day, from home (Level 4) → An admired expert (Level 5)"

**The Headline/Lead = Levels 4-5** (captures via deep desire).

**The Body = Levels 1-3** (explains how to achieve 4-5).

**APPLICATION #4 — Headlines Talk About DESIRE (Not the Product)**

**The rule (validated by Schwartz):**

**The Headline = the PRE-EXISTING DESIRE** (it captures the attention of those who ALREADY HAVE the desire).

**The Subhead/Lead = THE VEHICLE** (the product/method that achieves the desire).

**The pattern:**

❌ **Wrong:** The headline talks about the product (few people desire the product's name)

✅ **Correct:** The headline talks about the desire (many people have the pre-existing desire)

**Comparative examples:**

**Product: A Spanish course**

❌ Product headline: "Learn Spanish With Our Revolutionary Method"

Problem: Few people wake up desiring to "learn Spanish" (the words don't have a strong emotional charge).

Intensity: 4-5 (a casual interest for most).

✅ Desire headline: "Speak Fluent Spanish in 90 Days (Impress People on Your Trip to Barcelona, Get That Promotion at the International Company, Connect With Your Wife's Family)"

Pre-existing desires:

-   To impress on a trip (LF8 social approval, LF6 superiority)
-   A promotion (LF5 money, LF6 status)
-   To connect with family (LF7 loved ones)

Intensity: 7-8 (concrete, emotional goals).

The headline captures them via desires. The body reveals the vehicle = Spanish.

**Product: Productivity software**

❌ "TaskMaster Pro — Revolutionary Task Management"

Few people desire "task management" (a boring phrase).

✅ "Work 4 Hours/Day (Not 10) and Produce 2x More — Have Free Afternoons for Your Family/Hobbies While Your Colleagues Are Stuck at the Office Until 8 PM"

Desires:

-   To work less (LF5 comfort)
-   To produce more (LF6 superiority)
-   Time with family (LF7)
-   Freedom vs. stuck colleagues (LF6, LF8)

The headline = the desires. The body = TaskMaster Pro is the vehicle.

**Schwartz's rule:**

> _"Your headline has only one job: To stop your prospect._
> 
> _It stops them by appealing to their SELF-INTEREST — their existing desire._
> 
> _Not by mentioning your product name."_

#### **APPLICATION #5 — Connect Explicitly (A Transition Template)**

In the body copy, DECLARE the connection from product → desire:

**The template:**

"You want \[PRE-EXISTING DESIRE, validated\].

\[PRODUCT/METHOD\] is the vehicle that gets you there.

Here's how it works: \[a specific MECHANISM, 3-5 steps\]."

**Example (Copywriting course):**

"You want R$20k/month, working 6 hours/day, from home, without relying on a boss. \[The Desire — research validated that 87% of the avatar wants exactly this, Intensity 8-9\]

**Copywriting is the skill** that delivers this specific result.

Why? HIGH demand (every company needs copy — emails, ads, pages), LOW supply (few know how to write copy that converts), it's a REMOTE job (all digital, Zoom calls), and you ARE the expert (not an employee — clients pay you, you choose).

Here's how: \[The Mechanism — 4 steps\]

1.  Learn the skill (a 12-week course, part-time, 10h/week is manageable)
2.  Get your first clients (cold email templates, a portfolio with samples, strategic networking — taught in modules 8-10)
3.  Charge R$3-10k/project (pricing based on value, not hours — module 11)
4.  Scale to 2-4 projects/month (systemize, raise rates, get referrals — module 12)

The result: R$6-40k/month (depending on how many projects you accept), working 20-30h/week (the rest is your time), 100% remote, you control everything."

**An explicit connection:**

The desire (R$20k, 6h/day, home, autonomy) ← is connected via a clear mechanism → to Copywriting (the vehicle)

**The prospect sees a PATH** (not just a vague "this gives you that" — but a specific step-by-step).

**Collier:**

> _"Always enter the conversation already happening in the reader's mind._
> 
> _That conversation is about THEIR desires — not about your product."_

### **The "Create a Need" Fallacy (Addressing the Steve Jobs Objection):**

**A common objection (it always comes up):**

"But Steve Jobs said: 'People don't know what they want until we show them.'

He CREATED the desire for the iPhone (it didn't exist before).

Doesn't that contradict Axiom 2?"

**The answer (Schwartz/Axiom 2 remains valid):**

**Jobs did NOT create fundamental desires.**

**What Jobs did NOT create:**

❌ The desire for **music** (has existed for millennia — LF2 enjoyment, LF8 social, ancestral song/dance)

❌ The desire for **communication** (ancestral — LF4 companionship, LF7 family, LF8 social; humans are social animals)

❌ The desire for **information/knowledge** (a learned desire, but a strong one — curiosity, a survival advantage)

❌ The desire for **status/the cool factor** (LF6, LF8 — primate hierarchy, millions of years old)

❌ The desire for **beauty/design** (LF8 — aesthetic appreciation is universal)

❌ The desire for **simplicity/ease** (LF5 — effort minimization, evolutionary)

**What Jobs DID:**

✅ **CHANNELED** these (pre-existing) desires into NEW products (iPod, iPhone).

**The iPod (2001):**

**Pre-existing desire:** Portable music (anywhere, anytime).

**Proof the desire existed BEFORE:**

-   The Walkman (Sony, 1979 — 200M+ sold, 22 years before the iPod)
-   The Discman (1984 — 100M+ sold)
-   MP3 players (1990s — Diamond Rio, Creative Nomad, etc — an already established market)

**Jobs didn't create the desire for portable music** (it had existed for 22 years).

**Jobs channeled it BETTER:**

-   "1,000 songs in your pocket" (vs. 10-20 songs on a Discman)
-   Superior design (vs. clunky MP3 players)
-   The iTunes ecosystem (vs. a fragmented one)

**The base desire (music anywhere) was pre-existing.**

**The iPod was a superior channel** for an existing desire.

**The iPhone (2007):**

**Pre-existing desires:**

-   Mobile communication (cell phones since the 1980s)
-   Mobile internet (BlackBerry, Palm Pilot since the 1990s)
-   Mobile music (iPod since 2001)
-   Mobile photos (digital cameras since the 1990s)
-   Status/coolness (always — LF6, LF8)

**Jobs didn't create any of these desires.**

**Jobs CONVERGED them** (all of these desires into 1 device).

**Separate devices existed** (phone, PDA, iPod, camera).

The desire for each was pre-existing.

**The iPhone was the convergence** of multiple pre-existing desires into a single, superior vehicle.

**Even Apple, even Steve Jobs, obeyed Axiom 2.**

**No one escapes it** (not even geniuses).

Schwartz:

> _"The unsuccessful advertiser is the one who tries to CREATE desire._
> 
> _The successful advertiser is the one who CHANNELS existing desire."_
